}, \Lambda}\left(F_j-x_n^{(j)}\right)\\
				&= \liminf_{n \to +\infty} \mathcal {F}^{v}_{ C, \Lambda}(F_n^{(j)})
			\end{align}
			for all $j=1, \ldots, M_1$. By adding up in $j$ and setting $\mathbf{E}=(E_0, \ldots, E_{M_1})$, $\mathbf{C}=(C,C_1, \ldots, C_{M_1})$, we finally get that		
			\begin{equation}
				\mathcal{G}^{v}_{C, \Lambda}(\mathbf{E};\mathbf{C})\leq \liminf_n \mathcal{F}^{v}_{C, \Lambda}(F_n)=\inf\mathcal{F}^{v}_{C, \Lambda}(F)=\inf \mathcal{G}^{v}_{C, \Lambda}(\tilde{\mathbf{E}};\tilde{\mathbf{C}}), 
			\end{equation}
			where $F\subset \mathbb R^N\setminus C$ varies among all sets of finite perimeter and $(\tilde{\mathbf{E}}, \tilde{\mathbf{C}})$ varies among all possible asymptotic configurations for $C$: we have proved that $(\mathbf{E}, \mathbf{C})$ is an asymptotic minimizer for $\mathcal {G}^{v}_{C, \Lambda}$, so the thesis follows.
            
            \textbf{Step 3:} Finally, if $(\mathbf E, \mathbf C)$ is a generalized minimizer for $\mathcal{G}_{C,\Lambda}^v$, the existence of a sequence $(\tilde{F}_n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}$ with the properties stated in the Theorem follows by exactly the same argument as in Step 1. Indeed, since $(\mathbf E, \mathbf C)$ is a generalized minimizer, each $E_i$ is a $\Lambda$-minimizer for $\mathcal{F}_{C_i}$ and, by the density estimates in \Cref{prop: density est}, it is bounded. Therefore, the construction of Step 1 can be applied.
<!-- end proof-18 -->
<!-- lemma-10: body_md -->
\label{lm: concentration2}
			Let $a, b>0$. There exists $m=m\left(a, b\right)>0$ with the following property: if $E_1, \ldots, E_n\subset\mathbb R^N$ are sets of finite perimeter with $\sum_{j=1}^n|E_j|\geq a$ and $\sum_{j=1}^N P\left(E_j\right)\leq b$, then there exists $1\leq j\leq n$ and a cube $\tilde{Q}$ with sides of unitary length and parallel to the coordinate axes such that $|\tilde{Q}\cap E_j|\geq m$.
<!-- end lemma-10 -->
<!-- proof-19: body_md -->
Let $Q$ be the (open) unitary cube. If there exist $1\leq j\leq n$ and $z\in \mathbb Z$ such that $|E_j\cap\left(z+Q\right)|\geq \frac{1}{2}$, the thesis follow. If not, we have 
			\begin{equation}
				|E_j \cap \left(z+Q\right)| \leq |\left(z+Q\right) \setminus E_j|
			\end{equation} 
			for all $z\in\mathbb Z$. 
            
            Let $\gamma>0$ be the constant of the relative isoperimetric inequality, i.e.\ such that $|F\cap Q|^{\frac{N-1}{N}}\leq \gamma P\left(F, Q\right)$ for all sets of finite perimeter $F$ satisfying $|F\cap Q|\leq \frac 12$.
            Writing $V=\sup_{z\in\mathbb Z^N, \,j=1, \ldots, n} |E_j\cap\left(z+Q\right)|$, have:
			\begin{align}
				a&\leq \sum_{j=1}^n|E_j|=\sum_{z\in\mathbb Z^N}\sum_{j=1}^n |E_j\cap\left(z+Q\right)|\leq  V^{\frac{1}{N}} \sum_{j=1}^n\sum_{z\in\mathbb Z^N} |E_j\cap\left(z+Q\right)|^{\frac{N-1}{N}}\\
				&\leq \gamma V^{\frac{1}{N}}  \sum_{j=1}^n\sum_{z\in\mathbb Z^N} P\left(E_j, z+Q\right)\leq \gamma V^{\frac{1}{N}} \sum_{j=1}^n P\left(E_j\right)\leq \gamma V^{\frac{1}{N}} b.
			\end{align}
			This implies $\sup\limits_{z\in\mathbb Z^N, \,j=1, \ldots, n} |E_j\cap\left(z+Q\right)|\geq \min\left\{\left(\frac{a}{\gamma b}\right)^N, \frac{1}{2}\right\}$, which concludes.
<!-- end proof-19 -->
<!-- lemma-11: body_md -->
\label{lm: ef}
There exist $\varepsilon(N), c(N)>0$ with the following property. Let $E\subset\mathbb{R}^N$ be a set of finite perimeter with $|E|<v$ and let $B_R$ be a ball such that
$$
|E\cap B_{\frac{R}{2}}|\leq\varepsilon R^N,\quad |E\cap B_R|\geq \frac{\omega_NR^N}{2^{N+2}},
$$
with $\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon(N))$. Then there exists $\sigma_0=\sigma_0(E,v)>0$ such that for all $\sigma\in(0,\sigma_0)$ there exists a bi-Lipschitz map $\Phi_\sigma:\mathbb{R}^N\mapsto\mathbb{R}^N$, with $\Phi_\sigma(x)=x$ for all $|x|\geq R$, such that, setting $\widetilde E=\Phi_\sigma(E)$, one has
$$
|\widetilde E|<v, \quad P(E;\overline{B}_R)-P(\widetilde E;\overline{B}_R)\geq -2^NN\sigma P(E;\overline{B}_R), \qquad |\widetilde E|-|E|\geq c(N)\sigma R^N.
$$
<!-- end lemma-11 -->
<!-- proposition-8: body_md -->
\label{prop: esposito fusco unb}
			Let $\lambda\in(-1,1)$ and $v>0$. Then there exists $\Lambda_0=\Lambda_0(\lambda,v)>0$ such that if $\Lambda\geq \Lambda_0$, $C\subset \mathbb R^N$ is a closed convex set and $(\tilde{\mathbf{E}};\tilde{\mathbf{C}})$ is a generalized minimizer for $\mathcal G^{v}_{C,\Lambda}$, then $|\tilde{\mathbf{E}}|=v$. Moreover, the constants can be chosen so that $\Lambda_0(\lambda,v)=v^{-\frac{1}{N}}\Lambda_0(\lambda,1)$.
<!-- end proposition-8 -->
<!-- proof-20: body_md -->
The proof is based on an argument similar to \cite[Theorem 3.2, step 6]{FoFuLeMo23}. 
            For completeness, we provide the full proof.

			We argue by contradiction and assume that there exist a diverging sequence $(\Lambda_n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}$, a sequence $(C_n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}$ of closed convex sets, and for each $n$ a generalized minimizer $(\mathbf E^{(n)}, \mathbf{C}^{(n)})$ for $\mathcal{G}_{C_n,\Lambda_n}^{v}$ such that the volume constraint is not attained. In particular, let
            \begin{equation}
            \mathbf{E}^{(n)}=(E^{(n)}_0,\ldots,E^{(n)}_{k_n}), \qquad \mathbf{C}^{(n)}=(C_n,C^{(n)}_1,\ldots,C^{(n)}_{k_n}).
            \end{equation} 
			We observe that necessarily $|\mathbf{E}^{(n)}|<v$ for every $n$. Indeed, if $E_i^{(n)}\neq \emptyset$ for some $i$, one can cut $E_i^{(n)}$ with a hyperplane not intersecting $C_i^{(n)}$ in such a way that the volume remains larger than $v$, thus producing a competitor with strictly lower energy and contradicting minimality.

			By \Cref{cor: coercivity}, $\mathcal F_\cdot\geq \frac{1-\lambda}{2}P$.
            In particular, there exists $M>0$, depending only on $\lambda$, $v$ and $N$, such that
            \begin{equation}
            \sum_{j=0}^{k_n} P(E_j^{(n)}) \leq M
            \end{equation}
            for all $n$. 
            Indeed,
            \begin{equation}
            \sum_{j=0}^{k_n} P(E_j^{(n)}) \leq \frac{2}{1-\lambda}\mathcal G^{v}_{C_n,\Lambda_n}(\mathbf E^{(n)};\mathbf C^{(n)}) \leq \frac{2}{1-\lambda}P(\Omega_n)=:M,
            \end{equation}
            where $\Omega_n$ is a ball disjoint from $C_n$ with volume $v$ (notice that the value $P(\Omega_n)$ doesn't depend on $n$). Moreover, $\Lambda_n(v-|\mathbf{E}^{(n)}|)\leq M\frac{1-\lambda}{2}$, hence, for $n$ big enough, we have
            \begin{equation}
            \sum_{j=0}^{k_n} |E_j^{(n)}| \geq \frac{v}{2}>0.
            \end{equation} 
			
            By \Cref{lm: concentration2}, there exist a sequence $(j_n)_{n\in \mathbb N}$ and translations $(z_n)_{n\in \mathbb N}\subset\mathbb R^N$ such that $|(Q+z_n)\cap E_{j_n}^{(n)}|>m>0$ for some fixed $m=m(v,\lambda,N)>0$. Since $P(E_{j_n}^{(n)})\leq M$, up to subsequences there exist a closed convex set $\tilde C$ and $E\subset\mathbb R^N\setminus \tilde C$ such that $C_{j_n}^{(n)}-z_n\to \tilde C$ in the sense of Kuratowski and $E_{j_n}^{(n)}-z_n\to E$ in $L^1_{loc}$. In particular, $|E\cap Q|\geq m$ and $|\tilde C\cap Q|\leq 1-m$, hence $\partial^*E\setminus \tilde C\neq \emptyset$. Let $\bar{x}\in \partial^*E\setminus \tilde C$ and $\bar r>0$ be such that $\overline{B}(\bar{x},\bar r)\cap \tilde C=\emptyset$. By Kuratowski convergence, for $n$ big enough we have
            \begin{equation}
            \overline{B}(\bar{x},\bar r)\cap (C_{j_n}^{(n)}-z_n)=\emptyset.
            \end{equation}
            
            Fix $\varepsilon>0$. By the definition of reduced boundary, there exists $r=r(\varepsilon)>0$ such that, setting $E_r:=\frac{E-\bar{x}}{r}$ and $y:=-\frac{\nu_E(\bar{x})}{2}$, we have
        \begin{equation}
        \left|E_r\cap B\left(y,\frac{1}{2}\right)\right|<\varepsilon, \qquad \left|E_r\cap B\left(y,1\right)\right|>\frac{\omega_N}{2^{N+2}}.
        \end{equation}
        Up to decreasing $r$, setting $x_0=\bar{x}+ry$, it follows that
        \begin{equation}
        B(x_0,r)\subset B(\bar{x},\bar r), \quad \left|E\cap B\left(x_0,\frac{r}{2}\right)\right|<\varepsilon r^N, \quad \left|E\cap B(x_0,r)\right|>\frac{\omega_N}{2^{N+2}}r^N.
        \end{equation}
        Moreover, $\overline{B}(x_0 + z_n,r)\cap C_{j_n}^{(n)}=\emptyset$ for $n$ large. Hence the assumptions of \Cref{lm: ef} are satisfied for the balls $B_n:=B(x_0+z_n,r)$ and the sets $E_{j_n}^{(n)}$.
            
            
        For each $n$, choose $\sigma_n<\sigma_0\big(E_{j_n}^{(n)},\,v-|\mathbf{E}^{(n)}|+|E_{j_n}^{(n)}|\big)$ and let $\Phi_n:=\Phi_{\sigma_n}$ be given by \Cref{lm: ef}. Let $\tilde{E}_{j_n}^{(n)}=\Phi_n\left(E_{j_n}^{(n)}\right)$ and $(\tilde{\mathbf{E}}^{(n)};\mathbf{C}^{(n)})$ be the asymptotic configuration where we replace $E_{j_n}^{(n)}$ with $\tilde{E}_{j_n}^{(n)}$. Then

        $$
        |\widetilde {\mathbf{E}}^{(n)}|<v, \quad P(E_{j_n}^{(n)},\overline{B}_n)-P(\widetilde {E}_{j_n}^{(n)};\overline{B}_n)\geq -C(N)\sigma_n P(E_{j_n}^{(n)};\overline{B}_n), \quad |\widetilde {E}_{j_n}^{(n)}|-|E_{j_n}^{(n)}|\geq C(N)\sigma_n .
        $$
        Hence we have that
        \begin{align}
            \mathcal{G}_{C_n}(\tilde{\mathbf{E}}^{(n)};\mathbf{C}^{(n)}) + \Lambda_n(v- |\tilde{\mathbf{E}}^{(n)}|) &< \mathcal{G}_{C_n}(\mathbf{E}^{(n)};\mathbf{C}^{(n)}) + \Lambda_n(v- |\mathbf{E}^{(n)}|) 
            \\&+ C(N)\sigma_n ( P(E_{j_n}^{(n)},\overline{B}_n) - \Lambda_n )
        \end{align}
        Since $\Lambda_n \to +\infty$ and $P\left(E_{j_n}^{(n)}, \overline{B}_n\right) \leq M$, it follows that 
        \begin{equation}
            \mathcal {G}_{C_n, \Lambda_n}^{v}(\tilde{\mathbf E}^{(n)}; \mathbf C^{(n)})< \mathcal {G}_{C_n, \Lambda_n}^{v}(\mathbf E^{(n)}; \mathbf C^{(n)})
        \end{equation}
        for $n$ large enough, which is the desired contradiction. The dependence of the constants can be arranged in the stated form by a standard scaling argument.
<!-- end proof-20 -->
<!-- proof-21: body_md -->
The proof does not immediately follow by combining \Cref{teo: penal unb} with \Cref{prop: esposito fusco unb}, since we still need to construct an infimizing sequence $(\tilde{F}_n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}$ for the functional $\mathcal{F}_C$ satisfying the volume constraint $v>0$ and whose energy converges to the minimum of the generalized functional, as the one provided by \Cref{teo: penal unb} may not satisfy the volume constraint.
            
            Fix $\Lambda>\Lambda_0(\lambda,v)$ and consider the corresponding sequence $(F_n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}$ defined at the beginning of \Cref{section: existence}.
            Up to subsequences, as shown in Step 2 of \Cref{teo: penal unb}, 
            \begin{gather}
                \bigcup_{j=M_1+1}^{M} F_n^{(j)} \xrightarrow{n \to +\infty} E_0\quad \text{ in }L^1, \\
                F_n^{(j)} - x_n^{(j)} \xrightarrow{n \to +\infty} E_j \quad \text{ in }L^1 \text{ for every} \ j=1,\dots, M_1,\\
                C- x_n^{(j)} \xrightarrow{n \to +\infty} C_j \quad \text{ in the Kuratowski sense,}
            \end{gather} 
            where $E_j \subset \mathbb{R}^N \setminus C_j$ for every $j=1,\dots,M_1$, that $(\mathbf E, \mathbf C) = ((E_0,\dots,E_{M_1});(C,C_1,\dots, C_{M_1}))$ is a generalized minimizer for $C$ with volume constraint $v$ and that $\mathcal{F}_{C,\Lambda}^{v}(F_n) \to \mathcal{G}_C(\mathbf E, \mathbf C)$. 
            
            Let us now construct a sequence $(\tilde{F}_n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}$ that satisfies the volume constraint $v$. 
            Since $|\mathbf E|=v$, $\sum_{j=1}^k \big|F^{(j)}_n\big|-v$ is infinitesimal in $n$. Let us suppose that $\sum_{j=1}^k \big|F^{(j)}_n\big|>v$ for all $n\in \mathbb N$, as the other case is simpler. 
            
            Let us fix $j$ such that $E_j \neq \emptyset$.
            By \Cref{prop: density est}, \Cref{it: density est,it: perim est,it: height est}, there exists a sequence $n\mapsto B_n\Subset \mathbb{R}^N \setminus C_j$ of balls with infinitesimal radii such that, for $n$ large enough,
			\begin{equation}
			    \big|B_n\cap F_n^{(j)}\big|=  \sum_{i=1}^k \big|F^{(i)}_n\big|-v 
			\end{equation}
			and
			\begin{equation}
				P\left(F_n^{(j)}\setminus B_n;\mathbb R^N\setminus C\right)- P\left(F_n^{(j)};\mathbb R^N\setminus C\right)\to 0.
			\end{equation}
			Now, if we call 
			\begin{equation}
	        \tilde{F}_n=\bigcup_{\overset{i=0}{i\neq j}}^k F_n^{(i)}\, \cup \left(F_n^{(j)}\setminus B_n\right),
			\end{equation}
			we see that $|\tilde{F}_n|=v$ for all $n$ big enough. 
            Finally, 
            \begin{equation}
                |\mathcal{F}_C(\tilde{F}_n) -\mathcal{F}_C(F_n)|\leq P\left(F_n^{(j)}\setminus B_n;\mathbb R^N\setminus C\right)- P\left(F_n^{(j)};\mathbb R^N\setminus C\right), 
            \end{equation}
            which implies $\mathcal{F}_C(\tilde{F}_n) \to \mathcal{G}_C(\mathbf E, \mathbf C)$.
            This concludes the proof.
<!-- end proof-21 -->
<!-- proposition-9: body_md -->
\label{prop: gli E_i sono Lambda minimi}
        Let $C$ be a closed convex set and let $\lambda \in (-1,1)$.
        \begin{itemize} 
            \item If $(\mathbf{E};\mathbf{C})$ is a generalized minimizer for $\mathcal{G}_{C}$ with volume constraint $v>0$, then $E_i$ is a minimizer for $\mathcal{F}_{C_i}$ with volume constraint $v_i = |E_i|$ for all $i=0, \ldots, k$, where $k$ is the length of $(\mathbf{E};\mathbf{C})$.
            Moreover, for $\Lambda \geq \Lambda_0$ given by \Cref{prop: esposito fusco unb}, $E_i$ is a $\Lambda$-minimizer for $\mathcal{F}_{C_i}$ for $i=0, \ldots, k$.
            \item Let $\Lambda>0$ and $v>0$. If $(\mathbf{E};\mathbf{C})$ is a generalized minimizer for $\mathcal{G}_{C, \Lambda}^{v}$, then $E_i$ is a minimizer for $\mathcal{F}_{C_i, \Lambda}^{v_i}$ where $v_i=|E_i|$ for all $i=0, \ldots, k$, where $k$ is the length of $(\mathbf{E};\mathbf{C})$.
            In particular, $E_i$ is a $\Lambda$-minimizer for $\mathcal{F}_{C_i}$ for $i=0, \ldots, k$.
        \end{itemize}
        In particular, if $(\mathbf{E};\mathbf{C})$ is as in either of the two items above, then for $i=1, \ldots, k$, $E^{(1)}_i$ is open and $\partial^e E_i=\partial E_i^{(1)}$.
<!-- end proposition-9 -->
<!-- proof-22: body_md -->
Let $\Lambda>0$ and $v>0$ and assume that $(\mathbf{E};\mathbf{C})$ is a generalized minimizer for $\mathcal{G}_{C, \Lambda}^{v}$. 
        Let $i\in \{ 0, \ldots, k \}$, where $k$ is the length of $(\mathbf{E};\mathbf{C})$.
        Let $B$ be a ball and $F_i\subset \mathbb R^N\setminus C_i$ be such that $E_i\Delta F_i\Subset B$ and let $\mathbf F=(E_0, \ldots, E_{i-1}, F_i, E_{i+1}, \ldots, E_k)$.
        By minimality, 
        \begin{align}
            \mathcal{G}_{C, \Lambda}^{v}(\mathbf{E};\mathbf{C})&\leq \mathcal{G}_{C, \Lambda}^{v}(\mathbf{F};\mathbf{C})\\
            &=\mathcal{G}_{C, \Lambda}^{v}(\mathbf{E};\mathbf{C})+\mathcal F_{C_i}(F_i)-\mathcal F_{C_i}(E_i)+\Lambda\Big|\sum_{j\neq i} |E_j|+|F_i|-v\Big| -\Lambda\Big|\sum_{j\neq i} |E_j|+|E_i|-v\Big|\\
            &\leq \mathcal{G}_{C, \Lambda}^{v}(\mathbf{E};\mathbf{C})+\mathcal F_{C_i}(F_i)-\mathcal F_{C_i}(E_i)+\Lambda \Big| |E_i|-|F_i|\Big|
        \end{align}
        Since $E\Delta F\Subset B$, this implies 
        \begin{equation}
            \mathcal F_{C_i}(E_i; B)\leq \mathcal F_{C_i}(F_i; B) +\Lambda |E_i\Delta F_i|,
        \end{equation}
        proving that $E_i$ is a $\Lambda$-minimizer for $\mathcal F_{C_i}$.
        Similarly, from the second-to-last line, 
        \begin{align}
            F_{C_i}(E_i)+\Lambda \Big| |E_i|-v_i\Big|=\mathcal F_{C_i}(E_i)&\leq \mathcal F_{C_i}(F_i) +\Lambda \Big| |F_i|- |E_i|\Big|\\
            &=\mathcal F_{C_i}(F_i) +\Lambda \Big| |F_i|- v_i\Big|,
        \end{align}
        so $E_i$ is also a minimizer for $\mathcal F^{v_i}_{C_i, \Lambda}$.

        Assume now that $(\mathbf{E};\mathbf{C})$ is a generalized minimizer for $\mathcal G_C$.
        The fact that the components $E_i$, $i=1, \ldots, k$, with $k$ being the length, are minimizer for $\mathcal F_C$ follows from computations analogue to the above.
        Moreover, by \Cref{prop: esposito fusco unb}, for $\Lambda\geq \Lambda_0$, $(\mathbf{E};\mathbf{C})$ is a generalized minimizer for $\mathcal G^v_{C, \Lambda}$, where $v=|\mathbf E|$.
        By what has been proved above, then, $E_i$ is a $\Lambda$-minimizer for $\mathcal{F}_{C_i}$ for $i=0, \ldots, k$.
<!-- end proof-22 -->
<!-- corollary-6: body_md -->
\label{cor: le componenti devono toccare il convesso}
        Let $(\mathbf{E};\mathbf{C})=((E_0,\dots,E_k);(C,C_1,\dots,C_k))$ be a generalized minimizer for $C$ with volume constraint $v$. 
        Then, for every $i=0,\dots,k$ and every connected component $\Omega$ of $E_i$ the wet contribution of $\Omega$ is non zero, i.e.\ 
        \begin{equation}
            \mathcal F_{C_i}(\Omega)-P(\Omega; \mathbb R^N\setminus C_i) \neq 0.
        \end{equation}

        In particular, for every $i=1,\dots,k$, $\dim(C_i)\geq N-1$
<!-- end corollary-6 -->
<!-- proof-23: body_md -->
Suppose by contradiction that, for some $i$, $E_i$ has a connected component $\Omega$ whose wet contribution to the functional is zero.
        By \Cref{prop: gli E_i sono Lambda minimi}, $\Omega$ is a perimeter minimizer with volume constraint $w=|\Omega|$, and hence it is a ball. 
        
        Let then $(\tilde{\mathbf{E}}; \tilde{\mathbf{C}})$ be the asymptotic configuration with $\tilde{E}_j=E_j$ for every $j\notin \{0,i\}$, $\tilde{E}_i=E_i\setminus\Omega$
        and $\tilde{E}_0=E_0\cup B,$ where $B$ is an open ball contained in $\mathbb{R}^N \setminus(E_0\cup C)$ with volume $w$, such that
        either of the following hold:
        \begin{itemize}
            \item $\@ifnextchar^{{

}\cap C$ is a point of the reduced boundary of C.
        \end{itemize}

        Notice that $\mathcal G_C(\tilde{\mathbf{E}}; \tilde{\mathbf{C}})=\mathcal G_C(\mathbf{E}; \mathbf{C})$, so that $(\tilde{\mathbf{E}}; \tilde{\mathbf{C}})$ is a generalized minimizer for $\mathcal G_C$. 
        By \Cref{prop: gli E_i sono Lambda minimi}, for $\Lambda$ large enough $E_0\cup B$ is a $\Lambda$-minimizer for $\mathcal F_C$.
        
        Finally, it is easy to prove that, the complement $F=\mathbb R^N\setminus (E_0\cup B \cup C)$ is a $\Lambda$-minimizer for the capillarity energy with adhesion coefficient $-\lambda$.
        A computation shows that $F$ does not satisfy the density estimates of \Cref{prop: density est}, leading to a contradiction.

        In particular, for every $i=1,\dots,k$, $\dim (C_i) \geq N-1$ since otherwise the wet contribution of $E_i$ would be zero.
<!-- end proof-23 -->
<!-- remark-7: body_md -->
Let $(\mathbf{E};\mathbf{C})=((E_0,\dots,E_k);(C,C_1,\dots,C_k))$ be a generalized minimizer for the closed convex set $C$ with volume constraint $v$. 
        For each $i=0,\dots,k$ define $M_i:=  \overline{\bigl(\partial E_i\cap(\mathbb{R}^N\setminus C_i)\bigr)}.$
        Then there exists a closed set $\Sigma_i\subset M_i$ such that $M_i \setminus (C_i \cup \Sigma_i) $ is an analytic hypersurface and $\text{dim}_{\mathcal{H}}(\Sigma_i \cap(\mathbb{R}^N \setminus C_i) ) \leq N-8$. 
        Moreover:
        \begin{itemize}
            \item if $\text{dim}(C_i)=N$ and $C_i$ has $C^{1,1}$ boundary, then $M_i \setminus \Sigma_i$ is of class $C^{1,\frac{1}{2}}$ as a hypersurface with boundary. Moreover, \emph{Young's law} holds, namely, setting $B_i = C_i$,
            \begin{equation}
                \nu_{C_i}(x) \cdot \nu_{E_i}(x) = -\lambda \ \text{for every } x\in (M_i\setminus \Sigma_i) \cap B_i 
            \end{equation}
            \item if $\text{dim} (C_i)=N-1$, then $ M_i \setminus (\Sigma_i \cup \partial C_i)$ is of class $C^{1,\frac12}$, where $\partial C_i$ denotes the relative boundary of $C_i$ in the hyperplane $H$ containing $C_i$. Let $H^\pm$ be the open half-spaces determined by $H$, and let $\mu^\pm\in\mathbb S^{N-1}$ denote the unit normal to $H$ pointing into $H^\pm$. Setting $M_i^\pm:= \overline{(\partial E_i\cap H^\pm)}$ and $B_i = C_i \setminus \partial C_i$, \emph{Young's law} holds at every point in the relative interior of $C_i$, namely
                \begin{equation}
                \nu_{E_i}(x)\cdot \mu^{\pm}=-\lambda \qquad \text{for every } x\in  (M_i^{\pm}\setminus \Sigma_i)\cap B_i.
                \end{equation}
        \end{itemize}
        Finally, if $C_i$ satisfies the assumptions of either of the previous two cases, then $\Sigma_i \cap B_i = \emptyset$ if $N=3$, $\Sigma_i \cap B_i$ is discrete if $N=4$, and $\mathcal{H}^{s}(\Sigma_i \cap B_i) = 0$ for every $s>N-4$ if $N\geq 5$.
<!-- end remark-7 -->
<!-- corollary-7: body_md -->
Let $C$ be a compact convex set, $\lambda \in (-1, 1)$ and $v>0$.
        Then there exists a minimizer of $\mathcal F_C$ among sets $E\subset \mathbb R^N\setminus C$ with volume $v$.
<!-- end corollary-7 -->
<!-- proof-24: body_md -->
Let us start by noticing that $\mathcal K(C)=\emptyset$.
        Let $(\mathbf E, \mathbf C)$ be a generalized minimizer for $\mathcal G_C$.
        Then, by construction, the length of $(\mathbf E, \mathbf C)$ is 0, since $\mathcal K(C)=\emptyset$.

        By \Cref{prop: gli E_i sono Lambda minimi}, $E_0$ is a minimizer of $\mathcal F_C$ with volume constraint $v=|E_0|$.
<!-- end proof-24 -->
<!-- corollary-8: body_md -->
\label{cor: nanowires}
			Let $C\subset\mathbb R^N$ be a closed convex set such that all of its asymptotic cylinders have compact section. 
            Assume that for asymptotic cylinder $K$ for all $R>0$, there exist a diverging sequence $(x_n)_{n\in \mathbb N}\subset C$ and a sequence $y_n\in K$ such that there is $C\cap B(x_n, R)=\left(K\cap B(y_n, R)\right)+(x_n-y_n)$.
			Then for all $\lambda\in (-1, 1)$ and $v>0$ $\mathcal{F}_{C}$ admits a minimizer with volume constraint $v$.
<!-- end corollary-8 -->
<!-- proof-25: body_md -->
Let $(\mathbf E, \mathbf{C})$ be an asymptotic minimizer for the capillarity energy outside $C$, with 
			\begin{equation}
				\mathbf{E}=(E_0, \ldots, E_k), \qquad \mathbf{C}=(C,C_1, \ldots, C_k).
			\end{equation}
            
			Let $d=\max\limits_{j\geq 0}\operatorname{diam}(E_j)$ and let $D=\max\limits_{j\in \mathbb N}\operatorname{diam} \omega_j$, where $\omega_j$ is the section of $C_j$.
            Fix $R>2(d+D)$ and, for $j=1, \ldots, k$, let $(x^{(j)}_n)_{n\in \mathbb N}, (y^{(j)}_n)_{n\in \mathbb N}$ be as in the statement.
            
            Let $x_0\in E_0$ and $n_1, \ldots, n_k$ indexes such that $B(x_0, R)$ and $B\left(x_{n_j}^{(j)}, R\right)$ are all disjoint (it is possible to do so since the sequences $\left(x^{(j)}_n\right)_{n\in \mathbb N}$, $j=1, \ldots, k$ diverge).

            Notice that, by \Cref{cor: le componenti devono toccare il convesso}, $\@ifnextchar^{{

}_j\cap C_j\neq \emptyset$.
            By the invariance with respect to translations of the cylinders and the bounds on $R$, we can assume that $E_j\subset B(y_{n_j}^{(j)}, R)$ for $j=1, \ldots, k$.
			Let $F_0=E_0$, $F_j=E_j+x_{n_j}^{(j)}-y_{n_j}^{(j)}$, $j=1, \ldots, k$. We have that
			\begin{equation}
            \mathcal{F}_C(F_0) =\mathcal{F}_C(E_0), \qquad \mathcal{F}_C(F_j)=\mathcal F_{C_j}(E_j),
			\end{equation} 
			so, since by construction the sets $F_j$ are well-separated, $\mathcal F_C(F)=\mathcal G_C(\mathbf E;\mathbf C)$, where $F=\bigcup_{j=0}^k F_k$. 
            By \Cref{teo: generalized}, the thesis follows.
<!-- end proof-25 -->
<!-- definition-9: body_md -->
Let $C$ be a closed convex set.
    For $\lambda \in (-1,1)$, $v>0$, the isoperimetric profile $I_C$ associated to $\mathcal F_C$ is defined as 
    \begin{equation}
        I_C(v)=\inf \left\{ \mathcal{F}_C(E) : E \subset \mathbb{R}^N \setminus C,\ |E| = v \right\}.
    \end{equation}
    More in general, for $R>0$, we define the local isoperimetric profile as
    \begin{equation}
        I_{C,R}(v) := \inf \left\{ \mathcal{F}_C(E) : E \subset B(0,R) \setminus C,\ |E| = v \right\}.
    \end{equation}
<!-- end definition-9 -->
<!-- lemma-12: body_md -->
\label{lemma: first properties profile}
    Let $C$ be a closed convex set and $\lambda\in (-1, 1)$.
    Then for all $v>0$
    \begin{equation}\label{eq: asymptotic profile}
        I_C(v)=\min \mathcal{G}_{C}(\mathbf{E};\mathbf{C}),
    \end{equation}
    where the minimum is taken among asymptotic configurations of volume $v$.
    Moreover, for all $v>0$, $I_{C,R}(v)\to I_C(v)$ as $R\to \infty$.
<!-- end lemma-12 -->
<!-- proof-26: body_md -->
\eqref{eq: asymptotic profile} follows directly from \Cref{teo: generalized}.
    The convergence $I_{C,R}(v)\to I_C(v)$ follows from the monotonicity of $I_{C,R}(v)$ with respect to $R$ and the fact that the sets in the infimizing sequence constructed in the proof of \Cref{teo: generalized} are bounded for each fixed index. Hence, for each such set, its energy is attained for all sufficiently large $R$.
<!-- end proof-26 -->
<!-- proposition-10: body_md -->
\label{prop: isop profile del semispazio}
    Let $H$ be a half-space and $C$ be a closed convex set.
    Then
    \begin{enumerate}
        \item\label{it: profile flat} For all $v>0$, $I_H(v)=v^\frac{N-1}N I_H(1)$.
        \item \label{it: strict subadditivity} For all $v>w>0$ and $n\geq 2$, 
        \begin{equation}
            I_H(v) < \inf \{ I_H(v_1)+\dots+I_H(v_n) : v_1+\dots+v_n=v,\ v_i \ge w \ \forall i=1,\dots,n \}.
        \end{equation}
        \item \label{it: minimality} For all $v>0$, $I_H(v)\leq I_C(v)$.
        Moreover, if there exist $v>0$ and $E\subset \mathbb R^N\setminus C$ with $|E|=v$ such that $\mathcal F_C(E)=I_H(v)$, then $E$ is isometric to $B^\lambda[v]$ and it lies on a flat part of $\partial C$.
    \end{enumerate}
<!-- end proposition-10 -->
<!-- proof-27: body_md -->
\Cref{it: profile flat} follows from a simple scaling argument and the homogeneity of $\mathcal F_C$.
    \Cref{it: strict subadditivity} follows from the homogeneity and subadditivity of $I_H$.
    \Cref{it: minimality} follows from \cite[Theorem 1.1]{IsopCap}.
<!-- end proof-27 -->
<!-- proposition-11: body_md -->
\label{prop: proprietà del profilo isoperimetrico}
    Let $C$ be a closed convex set and $\lambda \in (-1, 1)$. 
    The following hold:
    \begin{enumerate}
        \item $I_C$ is strictly increasing and for every $a>0$ it is Lipschitz on $[a,+\infty)$, with constant $\Lambda_0(\lambda) a^{-\frac{1}{N}}$.
        \item If $v>0$ and $(\mathbf{E};\mathbf{C})=((E_0,\dots,E_k);(C,C_1,\dots,C_k))$ is a generalized minimizer for $I_C(v)$ there is a constant $H_{\mathbf{E}}$ such that the mean curvature $H_{E_i}$ of each $E_i$, $0,\dots,k$ is constant and equal to $ H_{\mathbf{E}}$ on $\partial^{*}E_i \setminus C$
        \item There exists a constant $\Lambda_0=\Lambda_0(\lambda)>0$ such that $0<H_{\mathbf{E}} \leq \Lambda_0 v^{-\frac{1}{N}}$.
        \item If $I_C$ is differentiable at $v>0$, then $I'_C(v)= H_{\mathbf{E}}$.
    \end{enumerate}
<!-- end proposition-11 -->
<!-- proof-28: body_md -->
The proof of Lipschitz continuity follows by the $\Lambda$-minimality. Indeed, let $a>0$, $v_1, v_2 \in [a,+\infty)$, $\Lambda_0 = \Lambda_0(1,\lambda)>0$ be the constant of \Cref{prop: esposito fusco unb} and let $(\mathbf{E}^{(i)},\mathbf{C}^{(i)})$ be the generalized minimizer for $I_C(v_i)$ for every $i=1,2$. By \Cref{prop: esposito fusco unb}, $(\mathbf{E}^{(i)},\mathbf{C}^{(i)})$ is a minimizer of $\mathcal{G}^{v_i}_{C, \Lambda_0 v_i^{-1/N}}$. Hence we have
    \begin{align}
        I_C(v_1) &= \mathcal{G}^{v_1}_{C, \Lambda_0 v_1^{-1/N}}\left(\mathbf{E}^{(1)},\mathbf{C}^{(1)}\right) \leq \mathcal{G}_{C}\left(\mathbf{E}^{(2)},\mathbf{C}^{(2)}\right) + \Lambda_0 v_1^{-\frac{1}{N}} \left | v_1 - \left| \mathbf{E}^{(2)} \right| \right | 
        \\&\leq I_C(v_2) + \Lambda_0 a^{-\frac{1}{N}} | v_1 - v_2|
    \end{align}
    Exchanging the role of $v_1$ and $v_2$ gives the Lipschitzianity on $[a,+\infty)$.

    Let $v_0 > 0$ and let $(\mathbf{E}^{v_0}, \mathbf{C}^{v_0})=((E_0,\dots, E_k),(C,C_1,\dots,C_k))$ be a generalized minimizer for $I_C(v_0)$. From the standard first variation theory for the perimeter under a volume constraint (see \cite[Theorem 19.7]{Ma12}), it follows that for every \(i=0,\dots,k\) such that \(E_i \neq \emptyset\), the scalar mean curvature \(H_{E_i}\) is constant on the free boundary \((\partial^{*}E_i)\setminus C_i\); denote this constant by $\lambda_i$. We prove that for every $i,j\in\{0,\dots,k\}$ such that $E_i\neq\emptyset\neq E_j$, $\lambda_i=\lambda_j$. We choose two vector fields $X_i\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R^N\setminus C_i;\mathbb R^N)$ and $X_j\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R^N\setminus C_j;\mathbb R^N)$ such that
    \begin{equation}
        \int_{\partial^*E_i\setminus C_i} X_i\cdot \nu_{E_i}\,d\mathcal H^{N-1}\neq 0, \qquad \int_{\partial^*E_j\setminus C_j} X_j\cdot \nu_{E_j}\,d\mathcal H^{N-1}\neq 0.
    \end{equation}
    Let $\Phi_i$ and $\Phi_j$ be the local variation generated by $X_i$ and $X_j$, namely
\begin{equation}
    \begin{cases}
        \frac{\partial }{\partial s}\Phi_i(s,x)=X_i(\Phi_i(s,x)), \\
        \Phi_i(0,x)=x,
    \end{cases}
    \qquad
    \begin{cases}
        \frac{\partial}{\partial t}\Phi_j(t,x)=X_j(\Phi_j(t,x)), \\
        \Phi_j(0,x)=x.
    \end{cases}
\end{equation}
For $s,t$ sufficiently small we define a new asymptotic configuration $(\mathbf E^{s,t},\mathbf C)$ by setting
\begin{equation}
    E_h^{s,t}:=
    \begin{cases}
        \Phi_i(s,E_i), & h=i, \\
        \Phi_j(t,E_j), & h=j, \\
        E_h, & h\neq i,j,
    \end{cases}
    \qquad C_h^{s,t}:=C_h \quad \text{for every } h=0,\dots,k.
\end{equation}
Since $\operatorname{supp}(X_i)\Subset\mathbb R^N\setminus C_i$ and $\operatorname{supp}(X_j)\Subset\mathbb R^N\setminus C_j$, this is an asymptotic configuration for $C$. Define
\begin{equation}
    V(s,t):= |\mathbf{E}^{s,t}| =|\Phi_i(s,E_i)|+|\Phi_j(t,E_j)|+\sum_{h\neq i,j}|E_h|.
\end{equation}
Then $V(0,0)=v$ and, by the first variation formula for the volume (see \cite{Ma12}),
\begin{equation}
    \partial_t V(0,0)=\int_{\partial^*E_j\setminus C_j} X_j\cdot \nu_{E_j}\,d\mathcal H^{N-1}\neq 0, \qquad \partial_s V(0,0)=\int_{\partial^*E_i\setminus C_i} X_i\cdot \nu_{E_i}\,d\mathcal H^{N-1}\neq 0.
\end{equation}
Hence, by the implicit function theorem, there exist $\varepsilon>0$ and a $C^1$ function $t:(-\varepsilon,\varepsilon)\to\mathbb R$ with $t(0)=0$ such that
\begin{equation}
    V(s,t(s))=v \qquad \text{for every } s\in(-\varepsilon,\varepsilon).
\end{equation}
Moreover,
\begin{equation}
    t'(0)=-\frac{\partial_s V(0,0)}{\partial_t V(0,0)}=-\frac{\int_{\partial^*E_i\setminus C_i} X_i\cdot \nu_{E_i}\,d\mathcal H^{N-1}}{\int_{\partial^*E_j\setminus C_j} X_j\cdot \nu_{E_j}\,d\mathcal H^{N-1}}.
\end{equation}
By the minimality of $(\mathbf E,\mathbf C)$, the function $s\mapsto \mathcal G_C(\mathbf E^{s,t(s)},\mathbf C)$ has a minimum at $s=0$, hence
\begin{equation}
    0=\frac{d}{ds}\mathcal G_C(\mathbf E^{s,t(s)},\mathbf C)\bigg|_{s=0}.
\end{equation}
Again, by the standard first variation theory for the perimeter contained in \cite{Ma12}, we obtain
\begin{equation}
    0=\lambda_i \int_{\partial^*E_i\setminus C_i} X_i\cdot \nu_{E_i}\,d\mathcal H^{N-1}+\lambda_j t'(0)\int_{\partial^*E_j\setminus C_j} X_j\cdot \nu_{E_j}\,d\mathcal H^{N-1}.
\end{equation}
Substituting the expression for $t'(0)$ yields
\begin{equation}
    0=(\lambda_i-\lambda_j)\int_{\partial^*E_i\setminus C_i} X_i\cdot \nu_{E_i}\,d\mathcal H^{N-1}.
\end{equation}
Since the last integral is non-zero, we conclude that $\lambda_i=\lambda_j.$ In particular, we may denote this quantity by $H_{\mathbf{E}^{v_0}}$.


\noindent We now show that, if $I_C$ is differentiable at $v_0$, then $I_C'(v_0)=H_{\mathbf{E}^{v_0}}$. This follows from a standard first variation argument (see \cite{Sternberg-Zumbrun} and \cite[Theorem 1.2, Step 4]{FuMo23}), which we briefly recall for completeness. Fix $i$ such that $E_i \neq \emptyset$ and consider a vector field $X$ such that $\text{supp}(X) \Subset \mathbb{R}^N \setminus C_i$ and 
\begin{equation}\label{eq: eq2 dim prof isop}
    \int_{(\partial ^{*} E_i) \setminus C_i} X \cdot \nu_{E_i} >0.  
\end{equation}
Consider the local variation $\Phi$ generated by the field $X$:
\begin{equation}
    \begin{cases}
        \frac{\partial \Phi}{\partial t} (t,x) = X(\Phi (t,x)), \\
        \Phi(0,x)=x. \label{eq: eq3 dim prof isop} 
    \end{cases}
\end{equation}
Let $\mathbf{E}(t) = (E_0,\dots, E_{i-1},\Phi(t, E_i), E_{i+1}, \dots, E_k)$ and set $f(t) = \mathcal{G}_{C}(\mathbf{E}(t),C)$. By the first variation formula, we have
\begin{equation}\label{eq: eq4 dim prof isop}
    f'(0) = \frac{d}{dt} P(\Phi (t,E_i),\mathbb{R}^N \setminus C_i)_{|_{t=0}} =  \lambda_i \int_{(\partial^{*} E_i) \setminus C_i} X \cdot \nu_{E_i}, 
\end{equation}
and 
\begin{equation}\label{eq: eq5 dim prof isop}
    \frac{d}{dt} \left|\mathbf{E}(t) \right|_{|_{t=0}} = \frac{d}{dt} | \Phi(t, E_i) |_{|_{t=0}} = \int_{(\partial^{*}E_i)\setminus C_i} X \cdot \nu_{E_i} >0. 
\end{equation}
Since the map $t \mapsto | \mathbf{E}(t) |$ has positive derivative at $0$, it is locally invertible. Denote its inverse by $t(\cdot) : v \in (v_0 - \delta, v_0 + \delta) \mapsto t(v) \in (-\epsilon, \epsilon)$ and define $\tilde{f}(v) = f(t(v))$. We have $\tilde{f}(v_0) = I_C(v_0)$, $\tilde{f}(v) \geq I_C(v)$ for every $v \in (v_0 - \delta, v_0 + \delta)$, and both $I_C$ and $\tilde{f}$ are differentiable at $v_0$. This implies that their derivatives coincide, hence
\begin{equation}
    I_C'(v_0) = \tilde{f}'(v_0) = f'(0)\, t'(v_0) = H_{\mathbf{E}^{v_0}},
\end{equation}
as desired. We also obtain that  
\begin{equation}
    H_{\mathbf{E}^{v_0}} \leq \Lambda_0(\lambda)\, v_0^{-\frac{1}{N}}.
\end{equation}
Indeed, arguing as at the beginning of the proof and using the $\Lambda_0$-minimality, we have
\begin{equation}
    |f(t) - f(0)| \leq \frac{\Lambda_0}{v_0^{\frac{1}{N}}} \, |V(t) - v_0| .
\end{equation}
where $V(t)= | \mathbf{E}(t) |$. Hence,
\begin{equation}
    |f'(0)| \leq \Lambda_0(\lambda)\, v_0^{-\frac{1}{N}}\, |V'(0)|,
\end{equation}
which yields the desired conclusion using \eqref{eq: eq4 dim prof isop} and \eqref{eq: eq5 dim prof isop}. 




\noindent We will now prove that $H_{\mathbf{E}^{v_0}} > 0$.
Let $x_0\in \partial^* C\cap \partial^* E_i$. 
For every $t\in \mathbb{R}$, define
\begin{equation}
    \Pi_t:=\{x\in \mathbb{R}^N : (x-x_0)\cdot \nu_C(x_0)=t\}.
\end{equation}
Let $t_*$ denote the first contact value for the translating hyperplanes, that is
\begin{equation}
    t_*:=\sup \{t\in \mathbb{R}: \overline{E}_i\cap \Pi_t \neq \emptyset\}.
\end{equation}
Then $t_*>0$ and there exists $y_0\in \Pi_{t_*}\cap \partial E_i \setminus C$. 
Moreover, by a standard argument, one can see that $y_0 \in \partial^* E_i$.
Indeed, suppose by contradiction that $y_0 \notin \partial^* E_i$. Then, blowing
up $E_i$ at $y_0$, by \cite[Theorem 28.6]{Ma12} we obtain, up to a
subsequence, a \emph{singular} minimizing cone $K$ (i.e.\ with
$\Sigma(K) = \partial K \setminus \partial^* K \neq \emptyset$). On the other
hand, since $E_i$ is contained in a half-space with $y_0$ belonging to the
boundary of that half-space, $K$ is contained in a half-space. This forces
$K$ to coincide with that half-space (see \cite[Theorem 15.5]{Giusti1984}),
hence $\Sigma(K) = \emptyset$, a contradiction. Therefore
$y_0 \in \partial^* E_i$.

By construction, $E_i$ lies on one side of $\Pi_{t_*}$ and touches it at $y_0$, hence by monotonicity of the mean curvature we have that $H_{\mathbf{E}^{v_0}} \geq 0$.
Moreover, if we assume by contradiction that $H_{\mathbf{E}^{v_0}}=0$, the free boundary $\partial^* E_i \setminus C_i$ is a minimal hypersurface. Hence, the strong maximum principle implies that $\partial^* E_i$ coincides with $\Pi_{t_*}$ in a neighbourhood of $y_0$. By analyticity of the regular part of the free boundary, this coincidence extends to the entire connected component of $\partial^* E_i \setminus C$ containing $y_0$. Thus this connected component of the free boundary is equal to $\Pi_{t_*}$, which is incompatible with the fact that $E_i$ has finite perimeter. In particular, since $I_C$ is almost everywhere differentiable and with positive derivative, we have that $I_C$ is strictly increasing.
<!-- end proof-28 -->
<!-- proposition-12: body_md -->
\label{prop: semicontinuity isop profile}
        Let $(C_n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}$ be a sequence of closed convex sets converging in the Kuratowski sense to $C$ and $(v_n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}$ be a positive sequence converging to $v>0$. Then 
        \begin{equation}
            \limsup\limits_{n \to +\infty} I_{C_n}(v_n) \leq I_C(v)
        \end{equation}
<!-- end proposition-12 -->
<!-- proof-29: body_md -->
Throughout the proof denote $B_r=B(0,r)$ for every $r>0$. Let $\Lambda > \Lambda_0(\lambda,1)v_n^{-\frac{1}{N}}$ for every $n\in\mathbb{N}$ where $\Lambda_0(\lambda,1)$ is the constant in \Cref{prop: esposito fusco unb}. Let $R>0$, from \Cref{cor: esistenza}, we have that there exists $E\subset B_R\setminus C$ such that
        \begin{equation}
            \mathcal{F}^{v}_{C,\Lambda}(E) = I_{C,R}(v)
        \end{equation}

}_{R}, \Lambda}^{v_n}(E_n) 
            \\ &= \lim_{n\to +\infty} \mathcal{F}_{C_n, \Lambda}^{v_n}(E_n) \geq \limsup_{n\to +\infty} \inf\limits_{F \subset \mathbb{R}^N\setminus C_n} \mathcal{F}_{C_n,\Lambda}^{v_n}(F) = \limsup_{n\to +\infty} I_{C_n}(v_n)
        \end{align}
<!-- end proof-29 -->
<!-- remark-8: body_md -->
\label{rmk: controesempio semicont isop}
    One might wonder if the semicontinuity proved in \Cref{prop: semicontinuity isop profile} could be improved to the continuity of the isoperimetric profile.
    This is certainly the case when the obstacles are compact and the convergence is in the sense of Hausdorff, thanks to \Cref{teo: gamma conv}.

    This is not the case, however, in general.
    Indeed, let $v>0$ and $C$ be a compact convex set whose boundary contains $B(0, R)\cap \{x_N=0\}$, let $(x_n)_{n\in \mathbb N}\subset \mathbb R^N$ such that $|x_n|\to \infty$ and let $C_n=C-x_n$.
    It is clear that, if $R$ is large enough, $I_C(v)=I_{C_n}(v)=I_{ \{x_N\leq 0 \} }(v)<I_{\emptyset}(v)$.
    On the other hand, $C_n\to \emptyset$ in the Kuratowski sense.
    
    \Cref{es: semicontinuity isop profile}, which relies heavily on the results of next section, gives an example in which the same lack of continuity happens even without loss of dimension.
<!-- end remark-8 -->
<!-- lemma-13: body_md -->
\label{lemma: density est gen min}
    Let $v > 0$. 
    Then there exist $c_0 = c_0(\lambda) > 0$ and $r_0 = r_0(\lambda, v) \in (0,1)$ such that the following holds. If $C$ is a closed convex set, $(\mathbf{E};\mathbf{C}) = ((E_0,\dots,E_k);(C,C_1,\dots,C_k))$ is a generalized minimizer with volume constraint $v$, and $\Omega'$ is a connected component of $E_i$ for some $i$, then
    \begin{equation}
        |\Omega' \cap B(x,r)| \geq c_0 r^N
    \end{equation}
    for all $x \in \overline{\Omega'}$ and $r \in (0, r_0)$.
<!-- end lemma-13 -->
<!-- proof-30: body_md -->
Let $\Lambda = \Lambda_0(\lambda, v)$ be the constant given by \Cref{prop: esposito fusco unb}. By \Cref{prop: gli E_i sono Lambda minimi}, $E_i$ is a $\Lambda$-minimizer of $\mathcal{F}_{C_i}$ for every $i\in \{ 0 , \dots , k\}$. 
    Hence, by \Cref{prop: density est} and the fact that $\Lambda$ depends only on $\lambda$ and $v$, the conclusion follows.
<!-- end proof-30 -->
<!-- proposition-13: body_md -->
\label{prop: bound componenti e diametro}
    There exist constants $L=L(\lambda)>0$ and $d=d(\lambda)>0$ such that if $C$ is a closed convex set, $v>0$, and $(\mathbf{E};\mathbf{C})$ is a generalized minimizer with volume constraint $v$, then the number of connected components of $(\mathbf{E};\mathbf{C})$ is less than $L$. Moreover, for every $i=0,\dots,k$, if $\Omega$ is a connected component of $E_i$, then $\operatorname{diam}(\Omega)\leq d\,v^{\frac{1}{N}}.$
<!-- end proposition-13 -->
<!-- proof-31: body_md -->
Up to rescaling, we may assume $v=1$. For every $i=0,\dots,k$, let $\Omega_1^i,\dots,\Omega_{l_i}^i$ be the connected components of $E_i$, and set
    \begin{equation}
        M=\sum_{i=0}^{k}l_i,
    \end{equation}
    which is the \emph{number of connected components} of $(\mathbf{E};\mathbf{C})$. By the density estimates proved in \Cref{lemma: density est gen min}, there exists a constant $w=w(\lambda)>0$ such that, for every $i=0,\dots,k$ and every $j=1,\dots,l_i$, if we fix a point $x_j^i\in\Omega_j^i$, then, setting $r=\frac{r_0(\lambda,1)}{2}$, we have
    \begin{equation}
        |\Omega_j^i\cap B(x_j^i,r)|\geq w.
    \end{equation}
    Therefore,
    \begin{align}
        1=|\mathbf{E}|=\sum_{i=0}^{k}|E_i|
        &\geq \sum_{i=0}^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{l_i}
        |\Omega_j^i\cap B(x_j^i,r)|\\
        &\geq \sum_{i=0}^{k}l_iw
        =Mw.
    \end{align}
    This proves the bound on the number of connected components. The estimate on the diameter follows from the density estimate proved in \Cref{lemma: density est gen min} by arguing as in \Cref{cor: stima sulle comp connesse e loro diametro}. The general case follows by scaling.
<!-- end proof-31 -->
<!-- lemma-14: body_md -->
\label{lem: lemma 4.3 Large volume}
    Let $C\subsetneq \mathbb R^N$ be a closed convex set such that $\dim (C_\infty) \geq N-1$. 
    Then there exists an asymptotic cylinder $K\in \mathcal K(C)$ whose boundary contains an hyperplane. 
    In particular, for all $\lambda>0$  
    \begin{equation}
        I_C(v) = I_H(v) \qquad \forall v>0,
    \end{equation}
    where $H$ is a half-space.
<!-- end lemma-14 -->
<!-- proof-32: body_md -->
If $\dim(C)=N$, the proof follows from \cite[Lemma 4.3]{RigLargeVol}, by noticing that the existence of such a $K$ is present in the proof of that Lemma. If $\dim(C)<N$, the obvious inequality $\dim(C_\infty)\leq\dim(C)$, together with $\dim(C_\infty)\geq N-1$, implies $\dim(C_\infty)=\dim(C)=N-1$.
        Without loss of generality, assume that $0\in C$ and that $C\subset \{x_N=0 \}$. Let $w\in{\rm int}(C_\infty)$ be an interior point relative to the hyperplane. Then there exists $r>0$ such that the $(N-1)$-dimensional ball centered at $w$ with radius $r$ is contained in $C_\infty$. Since $C_\infty$ is a cone, it follows that the $(N-1)$-dimensional ball centered at $nw$ with radius $nr$ is contained in $C_\infty\subset C$ for every $n\in\mathbb N$. Setting $x_n=nw\in C$, we therefore have $C-x_n\xrightarrow{n\to+\infty}\{ x_N=0 \}$ in the Kuratowski sense.
<!-- end proof-32 -->
<!-- remark-9: body_md -->
The statement above is slightly different from \cite[Lemma 4.3]{RigLargeVol}, which is only stated for $\dim C=N$ and does not mention the existence of such a $K$.
        The proof of the existence of $K$ is however contained in the proof of \cite[Lemma 4.3]{RigLargeVol}. The proof in the case $\dim C<N$ is immediate.
<!-- end remark-9 -->
<!-- remark-10: body_md -->
\label{rmk: caratterizzazione di d*(C)}
    In \cite[Remark 3.4]{RigLargeVol}, the authors give the following characterization: $d^{*}(C)$ is the smallest $k \in \mathbb{N} \cup \{ 0 \}$ such that there exist a vector subspace $Z \subset \mathbb{R}^N$ of dimension $k$ and a bounded set $B \subset Z^{\perp}$ for which $C \subset Z + B$, where $Z^{\perp}$ denotes the orthogonal complement of $Z$.
<!-- end remark-10 -->
<!-- lemma-15: body_md -->
\label{lemma: dim(K) <= d*(C)}
Let $C \subset \mathbb{R}^N$ be a closed convex set and let $\mathcal H= \mathcal{K}(C) \cup \{C\}$.
Then $d^*(\mathcal H)=d^*(C)$.
<!-- end lemma-15 -->
<!-- proof-33: body_md -->
Since $C\in \mathcal H$, $d^*(C)\leq d^*(\mathcal H)$. We will prove the other inequality. Throughout the proof, all limits of sets are taken in the Kuratowski sense. Let $K=\lim\limits_n t_n (C_n - x_n)$ where $(t_n)_{n\in \mathbb N}$ is a vanishing sequence, $(C_n)_{n\in \mathbb N}$ is a sequence in $\mathcal H$ and $x_n\in C_n$ for all $n$.
    Without loss of generality, assume $0\in C$. By \Cref{rmk: caratterizzazione di d*(C)}, we can find $Z$, a $d^*(C)$-dimensional subspace of $\mathbb{R}^N$ and a compact set $B \subset Z^{\perp}$ such that $C \subset Z + B$. 
    Since $C_n \in \mathcal H$ there exists a sequence $(y_h^n)_{h\in\mathbb{N}}$ in $C$ such that $C_n = \lim\limits_{h \to +\infty} (C - y_h^n)$. By the splitting above, we can write $y_h^n = z_h^n + b_h^n$ with $z_h^n \in Z$ and $b_h^n \in B$. Hence
    \begin{equation}
        C - y_h^n \subset Z + B - b_h^n \subset Z + (B - B).
    \end{equation}
    In particular, $C_n \subset Z + A$ for some compact set $A \subset Z^{\perp}$ and for every $n\in\mathbb{N}$. Again, writing $x_n = w_n + a_n$ with $w_n \in Z$ and $a_n \in A$, we obtain
    \begin{equation}
        t_n (C_n - x_n) 
        \subset t_n (Z + A - w_n - a_n) = Z + t_n(A-a_n) \subset Z + t_n (A-A) \to Z
    \end{equation}
    thanks to the boundedness of $A$.
    Therefore $K \subset Z$, and we conclude that $\dim (K) \leq \dim(Z) = d^*(C)$.
<!-- end proof-33 -->
<!-- corollary-9: body_md -->
\label{cor: caratterizzazione d*}
    Let $C$ be a closed convex set. 
    Then
    \begin{equation}
        d^*(C)=\max_{K\in \{C\}\cup \mathcal K(C)} \dim(K_\infty).
    \end{equation}
<!-- end corollary-9 -->
<!-- proof-34: body_md -->
First, by \Cref{lemma: dim(K) <= d*(C)} and the fact, observed before, that for a closed convex set $L$ it holds $d^{*}(L)\geq \dim (L_\infty)$, we have
    \begin{equation}
        d^*(C)=d^*(\mathcal{K}(C) \cup \{C\})\geq \max_{K\in \{C\}\cup \mathcal K(C)} \dim(K_\infty).
    \end{equation}

    Let us prove the opposite inequality.
    Let $(x_n)_{n\in \mathbb N}\subset C$ and $t_n\to 0$ be such that $t_n(C-x_n)\to K_{max}$ in the sense of Kuratowski with $d^*(C)=\dim(K_{max})$.
    If $(x_n)_{n\in \mathbb N}$ is bounded, $K_{max}=C_\infty$.
    Otherwise, up to subsequences, there exists $K\in \mathcal K(C)$ such that $C-x_n\to K$.
    Since $C$ is convex and $x_n\in C$, for $t>0$ and $n$ large enough, $t_n(C-x_n)\subset t (C-x_n)$.
    This implies $K_{max}\subset t K$ for all $t>0$, thus $K_{max}\subset K_\infty$, hence $\dim (K_\infty)\geq d^*(C)$, concluding the proof.
<!-- end proof-34 -->
<!-- theorem-10: body_md -->
\label{teo: principale d*(C)}
    Let $C$ be a closed convex set and $\lambda \in (-1, 1)$.
    Then the following properties hold:
    \begin{itemize}
        \item[(a)] Suppose that $d^{*}(C) \geq N-1$. Then there exists an asymptotic cylinder of $C$ whose boundary contains a hyperplane. As a consequence,
    \begin{align}
        I_C(v) = I_H(v) \ \text{for every }v>0 \label{eq: I_C = I_H}
    \end{align}
    where $H$ is a half-space, and every generalized minimizer of $I_C(v)$ is isometric to $B^{\lambda}[v]$, and sits on a flat part of the boundary of $C$ or on a flat part of the boundary of an asymptotic cylinder of $C$.
    \item[(b)] Suppose that $d^{*}(C) \leq N-2$. Then
    \begin{align}
        \lim_{v\to\infty}\frac{I_C(v)}{N\,\omega_N^{1/N}\,v^{(N-1)/N}} = 1\,, \label{eq: limite nel caso d*(C) < N-1}
    \end{align}
    Moreover:

    \medskip
    \noindent
    ($\text{b}1$) \textit{(Large Volume Regime)} There exists a constant $v_1(\lambda, C) > 0$ such that any generalized minimizer for $I_C(v)$ is connected for $v > v_1$.

    \medskip
    \noindent
    ($\text{b}2$) \textit{(Small Volume Regime)} There exists a constant $v_0(\lambda, C) > 0$ such that any generalized minimizer for $I_C(v)$ is connected for $v < v_0$.
    \end{itemize}
<!-- end theorem-10 -->
<!-- proof-35: body_md -->
We divide the proof into three steps.
    
    \vspace{0.2cm}
    \noindent
        \textbf{Step 1: proof of (a).}
    By \Cref{cor: caratterizzazione d*}, there exists $K\in \{C\}\cup \mathcal K(C)$ such that $\dim (K_\infty)\geq N-1.$
    By \Cref{lem: lemma 4.3 Large volume}, there exists $L\in \mathcal K(K)$ whose boundary contains a hyperplane.
    By \Cref{prop: i cilindri asint dei cilindri asint sono cilindri asint}, $L\in \mathcal K(C)$. Consider the asymptotic configuration $(\mathbf E; \mathbf C)=((\emptyset, E_1);(C, L))$, where $E_1 \subset \mathbb{R}^N \setminus L$ is a set isometric to $B^\lambda[v]$ that sits on the flat part of the boundary of $L$.
    By \Cref{prop: isop profile del semispazio}, this is optimal, proving \eqref{eq: I_C = I_H}.
    
    Let $v > 0$ and let $(\mathbf{E};\mathbf{C}) = ((E_0, E_1, \dots, E_k); (C, C_1, \dots, C_k))$ be a generalized minimizer for $I_C(v)$. For every $i = 0, \dots, k$, let $\Omega_{1}^{i}, \dots, \Omega_{l_i}^{i}$ be the connected components of $E_i$, and set $v_j^{i} = |\Omega_{j}^{i}|$. By the density estimate proved in \Cref{lemma: density est gen min} we have that there exists $w=w(\lambda, v)>0$ such that if $E_i \neq \emptyset$, then $v_j^{i} \geq w.$ By the isoperimetric inequality in \Cref{prop: isop profile del semispazio}, we have
    \begin{equation}
        I_H(v) = I_C(v) = \sum_{i=0}^{k} \sum_{j=1}^{l_i} \mathcal{F}_{C_i}(\Omega_{j}^{i})
        \geq \sum_{i=0}^{k} \sum_{j=1}^{l_i} I_H(v_j^{i})
        \geq I_H(v).
    \end{equation}
    Hence all inequalities are equalities and, by item~\ref{it: strict subadditivity} of \Cref{prop: isop profile del semispazio}, this implies that $(\mathbf{E};\mathbf{C})$ has only one connected component, say $\Omega = E_i$ for some $i$. Consequently,
    \begin{equation}
        I_C(v) = \mathcal{F}_{C_i}(E_i) = I_H(v).
    \end{equation}
    Using again the isoperimetric inequality in \Cref{prop: isop profile del semispazio}, this implies that $E_i$ is isometric to $B^{\lambda}[v]$ and lies on a flat part of the boundary of $C_i$.
    
    \vspace{0.2cm}
    \noindent
    \textbf{Step 2: proof of \eqref{eq: limite nel caso d*(C) < N-1} and (b1).} 
    The argument follows a strategy similar to \cite[Theorem 1.1]{RigLargeVol}.
    Using a ball as a competitor, we have that 
    \begin{equation}
        I_C(v) \leq N \omega_{N}^{\frac{1}{N}} v^{\frac{N-1}{N}},
    \end{equation}
    hence, to prove \eqref{eq: limite nel caso d*(C) < N-1}, it is enough to show that for any sequence $v_n \to +\infty$,
    \begin{equation}
        \liminf_{n \to +\infty} \frac{I_C(v_n)}{N \omega_N^{\frac{1}{N}} v_n^{\frac{N-1}{N}}}
        = \liminf_{n \to +\infty} \frac{I_{v_n^{-1 / N}C}(1)}{N \omega_N^{\frac{1}{N}} } \geq 1.
    \end{equation}
    
    Let $(\tilde{\mathbf{E}}^{(n)}; \tilde{\mathbf{C}}^{(n)})$ be a generalized minimizer of $I_C(v_n)$ and let $(\mathbf{E}^{(n)}; \mathbf{C}^{(n)})= v_n^{-\frac{1}{N}} (\tilde{\mathbf{E}}^{(n)}; \tilde{\mathbf{C}}^{(n)})$ be the corresponding rescaled generalized minimizer of $I_{v_n^{-1/N}C}(1)$. 
    By the uniform bound on the number of connected components given by \Cref{prop: bound componenti e diametro}, up to subsequences, we may assume that the length of $(\mathbf{E}^{(n)}; \mathbf{C}^{(n)})$ is $k\in\mathbb{N}$ for every $n$, and that the total number of connected components is $l \in\mathbb{N}$ for every $n$. 
    More precisely, let $(\mathbf{E}^{(n)}; \mathbf{C}^{(n)})=((E^{(n)}_0,\dots,E^{(n)}_k);(C_0^{(n)},\dots,C_k^{(n)}))$ and, for every $i=0,\dots,k$, let $\Omega_{i}^{1,(n)},\dots, \Omega_{i}^{l_i,(n)}$ be the connected components of $E_i^{(n)}$, where $\sum_{i=0}^{k} l_i = l$. Using again \Cref{prop: bound componenti e diametro}, we have that there exists $d=d(\lambda)>0$ such that $\text{diam} (\Omega_{i}^{j,(n)}) < d$. 
    Hence we can find $R>0$ and $x_i^{j,(n)} \in C_i^{(n)} = v_n^{-\frac{1}{N}} \tilde{C}_{i}^{(n)}$ such that $\Omega_{i}^{j,(n)} - x_i^{j,(n)} \subset B(0,R)$.
    Up to subsequences, there exists $\Omega_{i}^{j} \subset B(0,R)$ such that 
    \begin{equation}
        \Omega_{i}^{j,(n)} - x_i^{j,(n)} \xrightarrow[n\to +\infty]{L^1} \Omega_{i}^{j}
    \end{equation}
    Up to extracting further subsequences, we can also find closed convex sets $K_i^j$ such that 
    \begin{equation}
        C_i^{(n)} - x_i^{j,(n)} \xrightarrow[]{n\to +\infty} K_i^j.
    \end{equation}
    in sense of Kuratowski. By the definition of asymptotic dimension and \Cref{lemma: dim(K) <= d*(C)}, we have that $\dim (K_i^{j}) \leq d^{*}(\tilde{C}_i^{(n)}) \leq d^{*}(C)\leq  N-2$, so $\mathcal{F}_{K_i^j}(\cdot)= \mathcal{F}_{\emptyset}(\cdot)=P(\cdot)$. By \Cref{lemma: local hausdorff} and the $\Gamma$-convergence proved in \Cref{teo: gamma conv} we have that
    \begin{equation}
        \mathcal{F}_{K_i^j} (\Omega_{i}^j) \leq \liminf_{n \to +\infty} \mathcal{F}_{C_i^{(n)}-x_i^{j,(n)}}\left(\Omega_{i}^{j,(n)}-x_i^{j,(n)} \right).
    \end{equation}
    
    Putting everything together and using isoperimetric inequality, we conclude
    \begin{align}
        N \omega_N^{\frac{1}{N}} &\geq \liminf_{n \to +\infty} I_{v_n^{- 1/N}C}(1) \geq \sum_{i=0}^{k} \sum_{j=1}^{l_i} \liminf_{n \to +\infty} \mathcal{F}_{C_i^{(n)}-x_i^{j,(n)}}\left( \Omega_{i}^{j,(n)}-x_i^{j,(n)} \right) \geq \sum_{i=0}^{k} \sum_{j=1}^{l_i} \mathcal{F}_{K_i^{j}} (\Omega_{i}^j) 
        \\&= \sum_{i=0}^{k} \sum_{j=1}^{l_i} P (\Omega_{i}^j) \geq \sum_{i=0}^{k} \sum_{j=1}^{l_i} N \omega_N^{\frac{1}{N}} (v_i^j)^{\frac{N-1}{N}} \geq N \omega_N^{\frac{1}{N}}. \label{eq: stretta concavità implica connessione per volumi grandi}
    \end{align}
    This proves \eqref{eq: limite nel caso d*(C) < N-1}. Moreover, arguing by contradiction, suppose that there exists a sequence $v_n \to +\infty$ such that a corresponding generalized minimizer has at least two connected components. Repeating the argument above, by \Cref{lemma: density est gen min} there exists $w=w(\lambda)>0$ such that $\left| \Omega_{i}^{j,(n)} \right| \geq w$, hence $v_i^{j} = |\Omega_{i}^{j}| \geq w$. Therefore, using the strict concavity of $z \mapsto z^{\frac{N-1}{N}}$, the last inequality in \eqref{eq: stretta concavità implica connessione per volumi grandi} becomes strict, yielding a contradiction. This concludes the proof of (b1).

    \vspace{0.2cm}
    \noindent
    \textbf{Step 3: proof of (b2).} 
    We may assume that $C$ is a convex body.
    Indeed, if $\dim(C)\leq N-2$, minimizers are always connected, since they are balls.
    If $\dim(C)=N-1$, it is enough to choose $v_0$ sufficiently small so that a $\lambda$-spherical cap of volume $v<v_0$ can be accommodated on the flat part of $C$.
    Then, by the isoperimetric inequality in \Cref{prop: isop profile del semispazio}, all minimizers with volume constraint $v<v_0$ are of this form.
    
    Up to a translation and a rotation, we may assume that $0\in \partial^* C$ and that $C\subset H = \{ x_N \leq 0 \}$. Suppose by contradiction that there exist a sequence $v_n \to 0$ of volumes and a sequence $(\tilde{\mathbf{E}}^{(n)};\tilde{\mathbf{C}}^{(n)})$ of generalized minimizers for $I_C(v_n)$ with at least two connected components. 
    As before, let $(\mathbf{E}^{(n)};\mathbf{C}^{(n)})=v_n^{-1/N}(\tilde{\mathbf{E}}^{(n)};\tilde{\mathbf{C}}^{(n)})$ be the rescaled generalized minimizers and $C_n=v_n^{-1/N}C$. Note that 
    \begin{equation}
        C_n\to H \ \ \text{in} \ \ L^1_{loc} \ \text{and in the Kuratowski sense.} \label{eq: boh}
    \end{equation} 
    Again, by the uniform bound on the number of connected components from \Cref{prop: bound componenti e diametro}, up to subsequences we may assume that
    \begin{equation}
    (\mathbf{E}^{(n)};\mathbf{C}^{(n)})=((E_0^{(n)},\dots,E_k^{(n)});(C_n,C_1^{(n)},\dots,C_k^{(n)}))
    \end{equation}
    and that $\Omega_{i}^{1,(n)},\dots,\Omega_{i}^{l_i,(n)}$ are the connected components of $E_i^{(n)}$ for every $n\in\mathbb{N}$ and every $i=0,\dots,k$. In this way, the total number of connected components is $l=\sum\limits_{i=0}^{k}l_i$. Set $v_i^{j,n}=|\Omega_{i}^{j,(n)}|$; from \Cref{lemma: density est gen min} we have that there exists $w=w(\lambda)>0$ such that $v_i^{j,n}>w$ whenever $E_i^{(n)} \neq \emptyset$. From \Cref{prop: isop profile del semispazio} there exists $\alpha>0$ such that
    \begin{align}
    I_{C_n}(1)&=\sum_{i=0}^{k}\mathcal{F}_{C_i^{(n)}}(E_i^{(n)})
    = \sum_{i=0}^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{l_i}\mathcal{F}_{C_i^{(n)}}\left(\Omega_{i}^{j,(n)}\right)  
    \\ &\geq \sum_{i=0}^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{l_i}\mathcal{F}_{H}\left(B^{\lambda}\left[v_i^{j,n}\right]\right) > \alpha > I_H(1).
    \end{align}
    Our aim is now to construct a sequence $(F_n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}$ such that $F_n\subset \mathbb{R}^N\setminus C_n$, $|F_n|=1$ and
    \begin{equation}
    \mathcal{F}_{C_n}(F_n) \to I_H(1) = \mathcal{F}_{H}(B^{\lambda}[1]),
    \end{equation}
    yielding a contradiction. Let $B$ be the ball such that $B\setminus H = B^{\lambda}[1]$. 
    Since $|B|>1$ and $C_n\subset H$, by \eqref{eq: boh} we can find a sequence $(x_n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}$ such that $x_n\to 0$ and
    \begin{equation}
    |(B+x_n)\setminus C_n|=1.
    \end{equation}
    Let $F_n=(B+x_n)\setminus C_n$, and note that $F_n \to B^{\lambda}[1]$ in $L^1$. We now prove that the two parts of the energy converge:
    \begin{gather*}
    P(F_n,\mathbb{R}^N\setminus C_n)
     =\int_{\partial B+x_n}\chi_{\mathbb{R}^N\setminus C_n}(y)\,d\mathcal{H}^{N-1}(y) 
    \\= \int_{\partial B}\chi_{\mathbb{R}^N\setminus C_n}(y-x_n)\,d\mathcal{H}^{N-1}(y) 
    \longrightarrow \int_{\partial B^{\lambda}[1]}\chi_{\mathbb{R}^N\setminus H}(y)\,d\mathcal{H}^{N-1}(y) = P(B^{\lambda}[1],\mathbb{R}^N\setminus H).
    \end{gather*}
    For the wet part, since $\mathcal{H}^{N-1} \mathbin{\vrule height 1.6ex depth 0pt width
0.13ex\vrule height 0.13ex depth 0pt width 1.3ex}\partial^*(C_n-x_n) \xrightharpoonup{*}\mathcal{H}^{N-1}\mathbin{\vrule height 1.6ex depth 0pt width
0.13ex\vrule height 0.13ex depth 0pt width 1.3ex} \partial H$, we get
    \begin{gather*}
    P(F_n,C_n) = \mathcal{H}^{N-1}(\partial(C_n-x_n)\cap B ) 
    \\= \mathcal{H}^{N-1}\mathbin{\vrule height 1.6ex depth 0pt width
0.13ex\vrule height 0.13ex depth 0pt width 1.3ex} \partial^*(C_n-x_n)(B) \longrightarrow  \mathcal{H}^{N-1}\mathbin{\vrule height 1.6ex depth 0pt width
0.13ex\vrule height 0.13ex depth 0pt width 1.3ex} \partial H (B) 
    = P(B^{\lambda}[1],H).
    \end{gather*}
<!-- end proof-35 -->
<!-- corollary-10: body_md -->
\label{cor: I_C>I_H e esistenza dei minimi standard}
        Let $C$ be a closed convex set and $\lambda \in (-1, 1)$.
        Then 
        \begin{enumerate}
            \item if $d^{*}(C) \leq N-2$, $I_C(v) > I_H(v)$ for every $v$ sufficiently large, depending on $\lambda$ and $C$.
            \item if $d^{*}(C) \geq N-1$ and $v>0$,  $\mathcal{F}_C$ admits a minimizer with volume constraint $v$ in $\mathbb{R}^N \setminus C$ if and only if $\partial C$ contains a flat portion that can accommodate a spherical cap isometric to $B^{\lambda}[v]$.
        \end{enumerate}
<!-- end corollary-10 -->
<!-- proof-36: body_md -->
The proof follows immediately from \Cref{prop: isop profile del semispazio} and \Cref{teo: principale d*(C)}.
<!-- end proof-36 -->
<!-- example-2: body_md -->
\label{es: semicontinuity isop profile}
        Let
        \begin{equation}
            K := \left\{ (x,y,z)\in \mathbb{R}^3 : x^2+y^2 < 1 \right\}
        \end{equation}
        be the unit cylinder and define
        \begin{equation}
            C := \left\{ (x,y,z)\in \mathbb{R}^3 : x^2+y^2<1, \ 
            z \geq \frac{x^2+y^2}{1-x^2-y^2} \right\}.
        \end{equation}
        Then $C$ is a convex body contained in $K$ and unbounded in the $z$-direction. In particular, $C$ is trapped in the cylinder $K$ and therefore $d^*(C)=1$. For every $n\in \mathbb{N}$, set
        \begin{equation}
            r_n := \sqrt{\frac{n}{n+1}}
        \end{equation}
        and define the function
        \begin{equation}
            f_n(r)
            :=
            \begin{cases}
                \dfrac{r^2}{1-r^2}, & 0\leq r\leq r_n, \\[0.8em]
                n + f'(r_n)(r-r_n), & r\geq r_n,
            \end{cases}
        \end{equation}
        where $f(r)=\frac{r^2}{1-r^2}$ and $f'(r)=\frac{2r}{(1-r^2)^2}$. We then define
        \begin{equation}
            C_n := \left\{ (x,y,z)\in \mathbb{R}^3 : z \geq f_n\big(\sqrt{x^2+y^2}\big) \right\}.
        \end{equation}
        By construction, $C_n$ coincides with $C$ up to height $z=n$, while for $z\geq n$ its boundary is given, in each vertical plane containing the $z$-axis, by the tangent line to the graph of $f$ at $r=r_n$. 
        In particular, each $C_n$ is a closed convex set. It is immediate to see that $C_n \to C$ in the sense of Kuratowski. Finally, we claim that $d^*(C_n)=3$ for every $n$. Indeed, for $r\geq r_n$ the function $f_n$ is affine with strictly positive slope, hence $C_n$ contains a conical region of the form
        \begin{equation}
            \left\{ (x,y,z) : z \geq a_n \sqrt{x^2+y^2} + b_n \right\}
        \end{equation}
        for suitable constants $a_n>0$, $b_n\in \mathbb{R}$. This implies that the dimension of the recession cone of $C_n$ is $3$, and therefore $d^*(C_n)=3$.
<!-- end example-2 -->
